#### Exclave of Cabinda

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Exclave of Cabinda")]

Main article: [Cabinda Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinda_Province "Cabinda Province")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Governo_Provincial_do_Namibe_%2819543179475%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Governo_Provincial_do_Namibe_%2819543179475%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Governo_Provincial_do_Namibe_\(19543179475\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Provincial Government of Namibe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namibe_Province "Namibe Province").

With an area of approximately 7,283 square kilometres (2,812 sq mi), the Northern Angolan province of Cabinda is unusual in being separated from the rest of the country by a strip, some 60 kilometres (37 mi) wide, of the [Democratic Republic of Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_Congo "Democratic Republic of Congo") along the lower [Congo River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_River "Congo River"). Cabinda borders the [Congo Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_Republic "Congo Republic") to the north and north-northeast and the DRC to the east and south. The town of Cabinda is the chief population centre.

According to a 1995 census, Cabinda had an estimated population of 600,000, approximately 400,000 of whom are citizens of neighboring countries. Population estimates are, however, highly unreliable. Consisting largely of tropical forest, Cabinda produces hardwoods, coffee, cocoa, crude rubber, and palm oil.

The product for which it is best known, however, is its oil, which has given it the nickname the Kuwait of Africa. Cabinda's petroleum production from its considerable offshore reserves now accounts for more than half of Angola's output.[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-94) Most of the oil along its coast was discovered under [Portuguese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Angola "Portuguese Angola") by the Cabinda Gulf Oil Company (CABGOC) from 1968 onwards.

Ever since [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal") handed over sovereignty of its former overseas province of Angola to the local independence groups (MPLA, UNITA and FNLA), the territory of Cabinda has been a focus of separatist guerrilla actions opposing the [Government of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Angola "Government of Angola") (which has employed its armed forces, the FAA—Forças Armadas Angolanas) and Cabindan separatists.

### Foreign relations

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Foreign relations")]

Main article: [Foreign relations of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Angola "Foreign relations of Angola")

See also: [List of diplomatic missions of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Angola "List of diplomatic missions of Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Angolan_Foreign_Minister_Augusto_%2848578221376%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Angolan_Foreign_Minister_Augusto_%2848578221376%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Angolan_Foreign_Minister_Augusto_\(48578221376\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Foreign Minister of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_External_Relations_\(Angola\) "Minister of External Relations (Angola)") [Manuel Domingos Augusto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Domingos_Augusto "Manuel Domingos Augusto").

Angola is a founding member state of the [Community of Portuguese Language Countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_of_Portuguese_Language_Countries "Community of Portuguese Language Countries") (CPLP), also known as the Lusophone Commonwealth, an international organization and political association of [Lusophone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusophone "Lusophone") nations across four continents, where [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language "Portuguese language") is an official language.

On 16 October 2014, Angola was elected for the second time a non-permanent member of the [United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council "United Nations Security Council"), with 190 favorable votes out of a total of 193. The term of office began on 1 January 2015 and expired on 31 December 2016.[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-95)

Since January 2014, the Republic of Angola has been chairing the International Conference for the Great Lakes Region (CIRGL). \[80] In 2015, CIRGL Executive Secretary Ntumba Luaba said that Angola is the example to be followed by the members of the organization, due to the significant progress made during the 12 years of peace, namely in terms of socio-economic stability and political-military.[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-96)

### Military

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Military")]

Main article: [Angolan Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Armed_Forces "Angolan Armed Forces")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Luanda_-_Angola_%286276460346%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Luanda_-_Angola_%286276460346%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Luanda_-_Angola_\(6276460346\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

Soldiers of the [Angolan Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Armed_Forces "Angolan Armed Forces") in [full dress uniform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_dress_uniform "Full dress uniform")

The Angolan Armed Forces (Forças Armadas Angolanas, FAA) are headed by a Chief of Staff who reports to the Minister of Defence. There are three divisions—the Army (Exército), Navy (Marinha de Guerra, MGA) and [National Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Air_Force_of_Angola "National Air Force of Angola") (Força Aérea Nacional, FAN). Total manpower is 107,000; plus paramilitary forces of 10,000 (2015 est.).[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-97)

Its equipment includes Russian-manufactured fighters, bombers and transport planes. There are also Brazilian-made [EMB-312 Tucanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EMB-312_Tucano "EMB-312 Tucano") for training, [Czech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Republic "Czech Republic")-made [L-39 Albatroses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aero_L-39_Albatros "Aero L-39 Albatros") for training and bombing, and a variety of western-made aircraft such as the C-212\Aviocar, Sud Aviation Alouette III, etc. A small number of FAA personnel are stationed in the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo "Democratic Republic of the Congo") (Kinshasa) and 500 more were deployed in March 2023 due to the resurgence of the [M23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M23_rebellion "M23 rebellion").[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-98)[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-99) The FAA has also participated in the [Southern African Development Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community "Southern African Development Community") (SADC)'s [mission for peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community_Mission_in_Mozambique "Southern African Development Community Mission in Mozambique") in [Cabo Delgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabo_Delgado_Province "Cabo Delgado Province"), Mozambique.[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-100)

### Police

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Police")]

Main article: [Law enforcement in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Angola "Law enforcement in Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Angola_transito_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Angola_transito_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Angola_transito_\(cropped\).jpg)

Angolan National Police officers

The National Police departments are Public Order, Criminal Investigation, Traffic and Transport, Investigation and Inspection of Economic Activities, Taxation and Frontier Supervision, Riot Police and the Rapid Intervention Police. The National Police are in the process of standing up an air wing,\[*[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")*] to provide helicopter support for operations. The National Police are developing their criminal investigation and forensic capabilities. The force consists of an estimated 6,000 patrol officers, 2,500 taxation and frontier supervision officers, 182 criminal investigators, 100 financial crimes detectives, and approximately 90 economic activity inspectors.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

The National Police have implemented a modernisation and development plan to increase the capabilities and efficiency of the total force. In addition to administrative reorganisation, modernisation projects include procurement of new vehicles, aircraft and equipment, construction of new police stations and forensic laboratories, restructured training programmes and the replacement of [AKM rifles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AKM_rifle "AKM rifle") with 9 mm [Uzis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzi "Uzi") for officers in urban areas.

### Human rights

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Human rights")]

Main article: [Human rights in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Angola "Human rights in Angola")

See also: [LGBT rights in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Angola "LGBT rights in Angola")

Angola was classified as 'not free' by [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House "Freedom House") in the [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World "Freedom in the World") 2014 report[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-freedomhouse-101) and the 2024 report,[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-102) however the report has noted increases in freedoms under [João Lourenço](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Louren%C3%A7o "João Lourenço"). The 2014 report noted that the [August 2012 parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_legislative_election,_2012 "Angolan legislative election, 2012"), in which the ruling [Popular Movement for the Liberation of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Movement_for_the_Liberation_of_Angola "Popular Movement for the Liberation of Angola") won more than 70% of the vote, suffered from serious flaws, including outdated and inaccurate voter rolls.[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-freedomhouse-101) Voter turnout dropped from 80% in 2008 to 60%.[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-freedomhouse-101)

A 2012 report by the [U.S. Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_State_Department "United States State Department") said, "The three most important [human rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights "Human rights") abuses \[in 2012] were official corruption and impunity; limits on the freedoms of assembly, association, speech, and press; and cruel and excessive punishment, including reported cases of torture and beatings as well as unlawful killings by police and other security personnel."[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-103)

Angola ranked forty-two of forty-eight sub-Saharan African states on the 2007 Index of African Governance list and scored poorly on the 2013 [Ibrahim Index of African Governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Index_of_African_Governance "Ibrahim Index of African Governance").[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-104): 8  It was ranked 39 out of 52 [sub-Saharan African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa "Sub-Saharan Africa") countries, scoring particularly badly in the areas of participation and human rights, sustainable economic opportunity and human development. The Ibrahim Index uses a number of variables to compile its list which reflects the state of governance in Africa.[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-105)

In 2019, [homosexual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homosexuality "Homosexuality") acts were decriminalized in Angola, and the government also prohibited discrimination based on sexual orientation. The vote was overwhelming: 155 for, 1 against, 7 abstaining.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-106)

## Economy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Economy")]

Main article: [Economy of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Angola "Economy of Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Angola.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Angola.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Angola.svg)

GDP per capita 1950 to 2018

Angola has diamonds, oil, gold, copper and rich wildlife (which was dramatically depleted during the civil war), forest and fossil fuels. Since independence, oil and diamonds have been the most important economic resource. [Smallholder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smallholding "Smallholding") and [plantation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plantation "Plantation") agriculture dramatically dropped in the [Angolan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War"), but began to recover after 2002.

Angola's economy has in recent years moved on from the disarray caused by a quarter-century of [Angolan civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_civil_war "Angolan civil war") to become the fastest-growing economy in Africa and one of the fastest-growing in the world, with an average [GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP "GDP") growth of 20% between 2005 and 2007.[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-107) In the period 2001–10, Angola had the world's highest [annual average GDP growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_average_GDP_growth "Annual average GDP growth"), at 11.1%.

In 2004, the [Exim Bank of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Export%E2%80%93Import_Bank_of_China "Export–Import Bank of China") approved a $2 billion [line of credit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_of_credit "Line of credit") to Angola, to be used for rebuilding Angola's [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure "Infrastructure"), and to limit the influence of the [International Monetary Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund "International Monetary Fund") there.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-108)

China is Angola's biggest trade partner and [export](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Export "Export") destination as well as a significant source of [imports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Import "Import"). Bilateral trade reached $27.67 billion in 2011, up 11.5% year-on-year. China's imports, mainly [crude oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crude_oil "Crude oil") and diamonds, increased 9.1% to $24.89 billion while China's exports to Angola, including mechanical and electrical products, machinery parts and construction materials, surged 38.8%.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-109) The oil [glut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overproduction "Overproduction") led to a local [price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price "Price") for unleaded gasoline of [£](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pound_sterling "Pound sterling")0.37 a gallon.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-110)

As of 2021, the biggest import partners were the European Union, followed by China, [Togo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Togo "Togo"), the United States, and Brazil.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:3-14) More than half of Angola's exports go to China, followed by a significantly smaller amount to India, the European Union, and the United Arab Emirates.[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:4-15)

The Angolan economy grew 18% in 2005, 26% in 2006 and 17.6% in 2007. Due to the global [recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recession "Recession"), the economy contracted an estimated −0.3% in 2009.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIA-5) The security brought about by the 2002 peace settlement has allowed the resettlement of 4 million displaced persons and a resulting large-scale increase in agriculture production. Angola's economy is expected to grow by 3.9 per cent in 2014 said the [International Monetary Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund "International Monetary Fund") (IMF), robust growth in the non-oil economy, mainly driven by a very good performance in the agricultural sector, is expected to offset a temporary drop in oil production.[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-111)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Banco_Nacional_de_Angola_in_Luanda_-_Angola_2015.jpg/220px-Banco_Nacional_de_Angola_in_Luanda_-_Angola_2015.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Banco_Nacional_de_Angola_in_Luanda_-_Angola_2015.jpg)

The [National Bank of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Angola "National Bank of Angola").

Angola's financial system is maintained by the [National Bank of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Angola "National Bank of Angola") and managed by the governor [Jose de Lima Massano](https://no.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_de_Lima_Massano "no:Jose de Lima Massano"). According to a study on the banking sector, carried out by [Deloitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deloitte "Deloitte"), the monetary policy led by [Banco Nacional de Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banco_Nacional_de_Angola "Banco Nacional de Angola") (BNA), the Angolan national bank, allowed a decrease in the inflation rate put at 7.96% in December 2013, which contributed to the sector's growth trend.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-112) Estimates released by Angola's central bank, said the country's economy should grow at an annual average rate of 5 per cent over the next four years, boosted by the increasing participation of the private sector.[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-113) Angola was ranked 133rd in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2024.[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-114)

Although the country's economy has grown significantly since Angola achieved political stability in 2002, mainly due to fast-rising earnings in the oil sector, Angola faces huge social and economic problems. These are in part a result of almost continual armed conflict from 1961 on, although the highest level of destruction and socio-economic damage took place after the 1975 independence, during the long years of [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War"). However, high [poverty rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_rate "Poverty rate") and blatant [social inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_inequality "Social inequality") chiefly stems from persistent [authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism "Authoritarianism"), "neo-patrimonial" practices at all levels of the political, administrative, military and economic structures, and of [a pervasive corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Angola "Corruption in Angola").[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-115)[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-116) The main beneficiaries are political, administrative, economic and military power holders, who have accumulated (and continue to accumulate) enormous wealth.[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-117)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Cidade_Financeira_de_Luanda_2_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Cidade_Financeira_de_Luanda_2_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cidade_Financeira_de_Luanda_2_\(cropped\).jpg)

Luanda Financial City

"Secondary beneficiaries" are the middle strata that are about to become [social classes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_class "Social class"). However, almost half the population has to be considered poor, with dramatic differences between the countryside and the cities, where slightly more than 50% of the people reside.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

A study carried out in 2008 by the Angolan Instituto Nacional de Estatística found that in rural areas roughly 58% must be classified as "poor" according to UN norms but in the urban areas only 19%, and an overall rate of 37%.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-118) In cities, a majority of families, well beyond those officially classified as poor, must adopt a variety of survival strategies.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-119)\[*[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")*] In urban areas social inequality is most evident and it is extreme in Luanda.[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-120) In the [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index "Human Development Index") Angola constantly ranks in the bottom group.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-121)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Hotel_in_Lobito.JPG/220px-Hotel_in_Lobito.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hotel_in_Lobito.JPG)

[Tourism in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Angola "Tourism in Angola") has grown with the country's economy and stability.

In January 2020, a leak of government documents known as the *Luanda Leaks* showed that U.S. consulting companies such as [Boston Consulting Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Consulting_Group "Boston Consulting Group"), [McKinsey & Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McKinsey_%26_Company "McKinsey & Company"), and [PricewaterhouseCoopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PricewaterhouseCoopers "PricewaterhouseCoopers") had helped members of the family of former President [José Eduardo dos Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Eduardo_dos_Santos "José Eduardo dos Santos") (especially his daughter [Isabel dos Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isabel_dos_Santos "Isabel dos Santos")) corruptly run Sonangol for their own personal profit, helping them use the company's revenues to fund vanity projects in France and Switzerland.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-122) After further revelations in the [Pandora Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandora_Papers "Pandora Papers"), former generals [Dias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A9lder_Vieira_Dias "Hélder Vieira Dias") and [do Nascimento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopoldino_Fragoso_do_Nascimento "Leopoldino Fragoso do Nascimento") and former presidential advisers were also accused of misappropriating significant public funds for personal benefit.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-fit1-123)

The enormous differences between the regions pose a serious structural problem for the Angolan economy, illustrated by the fact that about one third of economic activities are concentrated in Luanda and neighbouring Bengo province, while several areas of the interior suffer economic stagnation and even regression.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-124)

One of the economic consequences of social and regional disparities is a sharp increase in Angolan private investments abroad. The small fringe of Angolan society where most of the asset accumulation takes place seeks to spread its assets, for reasons of security and profit. For the time being, the biggest share of these investments is concentrated in Portugal where the [Angolan presence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolans_in_Portugal "Angolans in Portugal") (including the family of the state president) in banks as well as in the domains of energy, [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Angola "Telecommunications in Angola"), and [mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media "Mass media") has become notable, as has the acquisition of vineyards and orchards as well as of tourism enterprises.[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-125)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Rua_Kwame_Nkrumah_Luanda_05.JPG/220px-Rua_Kwame_Nkrumah_Luanda_05.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rua_Kwame_Nkrumah_Luanda_05.JPG)

Corporate headquarters in Luanda

Angola has upgraded critical infrastructure, an investment made possible by funds from the country's development of oil resources.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-126) According to a report, just slightly more than ten years after the end of the [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War") Angola's standard of living has overall greatly improved. Life expectancy, which was just 46 years in 2002, reached 51 in 2011. Mortality rates for children fell from 25 per cent in 2001 to 19 per cent in 2010 and the number of students enrolled in primary school has tripled since 2001.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-127) However, at the same time the social and economic inequality that has characterised the country for so long has not diminished, but has deepened in all respects.

With a stock of assets corresponding to 70 billion Kz (US$6.8 billion), Angola is now the third-largest financial market in sub-Saharan Africa, surpassed only by [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria") and South Africa. According to the Angolan Minister of Economy, [Abraão Gourgel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abra%C3%A3o_Gourgel "Abraão Gourgel"), the financial market of the country grew modestly since 2002 and now occupies third place in sub-Saharan Africa.[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)

On 19 December 2014, the Capital Market in Angola was launched. [BODIVA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola_Stock_Exchange_and_Derivatives "Angola Stock Exchange and Derivatives") (Angola Stock Exchange and Derivatives, in English) was allocated the secondary public debt market, and was expected to launch the corporate debt market by 2015, though the stock market itself was only expected to commence trading in 2016.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129)

### Natural resources

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Natural resources")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Offshore_platform_on_move_to_final_destination%2C_Ilha_de_Luanda_%28cropped%29.JPG/220px-Offshore_platform_on_move_to_final_destination%2C_Ilha_de_Luanda_%28cropped%29.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Offshore_platform_on_move_to_final_destination,_Ilha_de_Luanda_\(cropped\).JPG)

An [offshore oil drilling platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform "Oil platform") off the coast of central Angola

*[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist "The Economist")* reported in 2008 that diamonds and oil make up 60% of Angola's economy, almost all of the country's [revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue "Revenue") and all of its dominant [exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Export "Export").[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-130) Growth is almost entirely driven by rising [oil production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraction_of_petroleum "Extraction of petroleum") which surpassed 1.4 million barrels per day (220,000 m3/d) in late 2005 and was expected to grow to 2 million barrels per day (320,000 m3/d) by 2007. Control of the [oil industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_industry "Petroleum industry") is consolidated in [Sonangol Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonangol_Group "Sonangol Group"), a conglomerate owned by the Angolan government. In December 2006, Angola was admitted as a member of [OPEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OPEC "OPEC").[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-131) In 2022, the country produced an average of 1.165 million barrels of oil per day, according to *Agência Nacional de Petróleo, Gás e Biocombustíveis* (ANPG), the national oil, gas and biofuels agency.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-132)

According to [the Heritage Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Heritage_Foundation "The Heritage Foundation"), a conservative American [think tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Think_tank "Think tank"), oil production from Angola has increased so significantly that Angola now is China's biggest supplier of oil.[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-133) "China has extended three multi-billion dollar lines of credit to the Angolan government; two loans of $2 billion from China Exim Bank, one in 2004, the second in 2007, as well as one loan in 2005 of $2.9 billion from [China International Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_International_Fund "China International Fund") Ltd."[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-134)

Growing oil revenues also created opportunities for [corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Angola "Corruption in Angola"): according to a recent [Human Rights Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch "Human Rights Watch") report, US$32 billion disappeared from government accounts in 2007–2010.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-135) Furthermore, [Sonangol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonangol "Sonangol"), the state-run oil company, controls 51% of Cabinda's oil. Due to this market control, the company ends up determining the profit received by the government and the taxes it pays. The council of foreign affairs states that the World Bank mentioned that Sonangol is a taxpayer, it carries out quasi-fiscal activities, it invests public funds, and, as concessionaire, it is a sector regulator. This multifarious work program creates conflicts of interest and characterises a complex relationship between Sonangol and the government that weakens the formal budgetary process and creates uncertainty as regards the actual fiscal stance of the state."[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-136)

In 2002, Angola demanded compensation for [oil spills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_spill "Oil spill") allegedly caused by [Chevron Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chevron_Corporation "Chevron Corporation"), the first time it had fined a multinational corporation operating in its waters.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-137)

Operations in its [diamond mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diamond_mines "List of diamond mines") include partnerships between state-run [Endiama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endiama "Endiama") and mining companies such as [ALROSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ALROSA "ALROSA") which operate in Angola.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-138)

Access to [biocapacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biocapacity "Biocapacity") in Angola is higher than world average. In 2016, Angola had 1.9 global hectares[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-139) of biocapacity per person within its territory, slightly more than world average of 1.6 global hectares per person.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-140) In 2016, Angola used 1.01 global hectares of biocapacity per person – their [ecological footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecological_footprint "Ecological footprint") of consumption. This means they use about half as much biocapacity as Angola contains. As a result, Angola is running a biocapacity reserve.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-139)

### Agriculture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Agriculture")]

Main article: [Agriculture in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Angola "Agriculture in Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/HPP_%22CAPANDA%22.jpg/220px-HPP_%22CAPANDA%22.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:HPP_%22CAPANDA%22.jpg)

[Capanda Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capanda_Dam "Capanda Dam") on the [Cuanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuanza_River "Cuanza River")

Agriculture and forestry is an area of potential opportunity for the country. The African Economic Outlook organization states that "Angola requires 4.5 million tonnes a year of grain but grows only about 55% of the [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize "Maize") it needs, 20% of the [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice "Rice") and just 5% of its required [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat "Wheat")".[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-141)

In addition, the World Bank estimates that "less than 3 per cent of Angola's abundant fertile land is cultivated and the economic potential of the forestry sector remains largely unexploited".[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-142)

Before independence in 1975, [Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola_\(Portugal\) "Angola (Portugal)") was a bread-basket of southern Africa and a major exporter of [bananas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana "Banana"), coffee and [sisal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sisal "Sisal"), but [three decades of civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War") destroyed fertile countryside, left it littered with [landmines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landmine "Landmine") and drove millions into the cities. The country now depends on expensive food imports, mainly from South Africa and [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal"), while more than 90% of farming is done at the family and subsistence level. Thousands of Angolan small-scale farmers are trapped in poverty.[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-143)

### Transport

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Transport")]

Main article: [Transport in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Angola "Transport in Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/TAAG_Angola_Airlines_Boeing_737-200Adv_D2-TBD_CDG_July_1986.png/220px-TAAG_Angola_Airlines_Boeing_737-200Adv_D2-TBD_CDG_July_1986.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:TAAG_Angola_Airlines_Boeing_737-200Adv_D2-TBD_CDG_July_1986.png)

[TAAG Angola Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAAG_Angola_Airlines "TAAG Angola Airlines") is the country's state-owned [national carrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_carrier "National carrier").

Transport in Angola consists of:

- Three separate [railway systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Angola "Rail transport in Angola") totalling 2,761 km (1,716 mi)
- 76,626 km (47,613 mi) of highway of which 19,156 km (11,903 mi) is paved
- 1,295 navigable inland waterways
- five major [sea ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_port "Sea port")
- 243 [airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Angola "List of airports in Angola"), of which 32 are paved.

Angola centers its port trade in five main ports: [Namibe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Namibe "Port of Namibe"), [Lobito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Lobito "Port of Lobito"), [Soyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soyo "Soyo"), [Cabinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinda_\(city\) "Cabinda (city)") and Luanda. The [port of Luanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Luanda "Port of Luanda") is the largest of the five, as well as being one of the busiest on the African continent.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-africanbusinesscentral.com-144)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Catumbela_River_Bridge_%2819478241049%29.jpg/220px-Catumbela_River_Bridge_%2819478241049%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Catumbela_River_Bridge_\(19478241049\).jpg)

[Catumbela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catumbela "Catumbela") Bridge in [Benguela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benguela_Province "Benguela Province").

Two trans-African automobile routes pass through Angola: the [Tripoli-Cape Town Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripoli-Cape_Town_Highway "Tripoli-Cape Town Highway") and the [Beira-Lobito Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beira-Lobito_Highway "Beira-Lobito Highway"). Travel on highways outside of towns and cities in Angola (and in some cases within) is\[*[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")*] often not best advised for those without four-by-four vehicles. While reasonable road infrastructure has existed within Angola, time and war have taken their toll on the road surfaces, leaving many severely potholed, littered with broken asphalt. In many areas drivers have established alternative tracks to avoid the worst parts of the surface, although careful attention must be paid to the presence or absence of landmine warning markers by the side of the road. The [Angolan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_government "Angolan government") has contracted the restoration of many of the country's roads. The road between Lubango and Namibe, for example, was completed recently with funding from the European Union,[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-145) and is comparable to many European main routes. Completing the road infrastructure is likely to take some decades, but substantial efforts are already being made.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

The old airport in Luanda, [Quatro de Fevereiro Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quatro_de_Fevereiro_Airport "Quatro de Fevereiro Airport"), will be replaced by the new [Dr. Antonio Agostinho Neto International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Antonio_Agostinho_Neto_International_Airport "Dr. Antonio Agostinho Neto International Airport").

### Telecommunications

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Telecommunications")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Porto_do_Lobito_-_Angola_2015_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Porto_do_Lobito_-_Angola_2015_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Porto_do_Lobito_-_Angola_2015_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Lobito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobito "Lobito") hosts a major seaport.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Marginal_Avenida_4_de_Fevreiro_Luanda_March_2013_13_%28cropped%29.JPG/220px-Marginal_Avenida_4_de_Fevreiro_Luanda_March_2013_13_%28cropped%29.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Marginal_Avenida_4_de_Fevreiro_Luanda_March_2013_13_\(cropped\).JPG)

[Luanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luanda "Luanda")'s construction boom is financed largely by oil and diamonds.

The [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications "Telecommunications") industry is considered one of the main strategic sectors in Angola.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-146)

For additional, see [Telephone numbers in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_Angola "Telephone numbers in Angola").

In October 2014, the building of an [optic fiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optic_fiber "Optic fiber") underwater cable was announced.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-147) This project aims to turn Angola into a continental hub, thus improving Internet connections both nationally and internationally.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-148)

On 11 March 2015, the First Angolan Forum of Telecommunications and Information Technology was held in [Luanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luanda "Luanda") under the motto "The challenges of telecommunications in the current context of Angola",[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-149) to promote debate on topical issues on [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications "Telecommunications") in Angola and worldwide.[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-150) A study of this sector, presented at the forum, said Angola had the first telecommunications operator in Africa to test LTE – with speeds up to 400 Mbit/s – and mobile penetration of about 75%; there are about 3.5 million [smartphones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smartphone "Smartphone") in the Angolan market; There are about 25,000 kilometres (16,000 miles) of optical fibre installed in the country.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-151)[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-152)

The first Angolan satellite, [AngoSat-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angosat_1 "Angosat 1"), was launched into orbit on 26 December 2017.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-153) It was launched from the [Baikonur space center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baikonur_Cosmodrome "Baikonur Cosmodrome") in Kazakhstan on board a [Zenit 3F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenit-3F "Zenit-3F") rocket. The [satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite "Satellite") was built by Russia's [RSC Energia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RSC_Energia "RSC Energia"), a subsidiary of the state-run space industry player [Roscosmos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roscosmos "Roscosmos"). The satellite payload was supplied by [Airbus Defence & Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_Defence_%26_Space "Airbus Defence & Space").[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-154) Due to an on-board power failure during solar panel deployment, on 27 December, RSC Energia revealed that they lost communications contact with the satellite. Although, subsequent attempts to restore communications with the satellite were successful, the satellite eventually stopped sending data and RSC Energia confirmed that AngoSat-1 was inoperable. The launch of AngoSat-1 was aimed at ensuring telecommunications throughout the country.[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-155) According to Aristides Safeca, Secretary of State for Telecommunications, the satellite was aimed at providing telecommunications services, TV, internet and [e-government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-government "E-government") and was expected to remain in operation "at best" for 18 years.[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-156)

A replacement satellite named [AngoSat-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AngoSat_2 "AngoSat 2") was pursued and was expected to be in service by 2020.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-157) As of February 2021, Ango-Sat-2 was about 60% ready. The officials reported the launch was expected in about 17 months, by July 2022.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-158) The launch of AngoSat-2 occurred on 12 October 2022.[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-q752-159)

### Technology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Technology")]

The management of the [top-level domain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top-level_domain "Top-level domain") '[.ao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/.ao ".ao")' passed from [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal") to Angola in 2015, following new legislation.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-160) A joint decree of Minister of Telecommunications and Information Technologies José Carvalho da Rocha and the minister of Science and Technology, Maria Cândida Pereira Teixeira, states that "under the massification" of that Angolan domain, "conditions are created for the transfer of the domain root '.ao' of Portugal to Angola".[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-161)

## Demographics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Demographics")]

Main article: [Demographics of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Angola "Demographics of Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Angola_Ethnic_map_1970.svg/260px-Angola_Ethnic_map_1970.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Angola_Ethnic_map_1970.svg)

Historical ethnic divisions of Angola

Angola has a population of 24,383,301 inhabitants according to the preliminary results of its 2014 census, the first one conducted or carried out since 15 December 1970.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-INE_Angola-162) It is composed of [Ovimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovimbundu "Ovimbundu") (language [Umbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbundu "Umbundu")) 37%, [Ambundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Mbundu_people "Northern Mbundu people") (language [Kimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbundu "Kimbundu")) 23%, [Bakongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakongo "Bakongo") 13%, and 32% other ethnic groups (including the [Chokwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chokwe_people "Chokwe people"), the [Ovambo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovambo_people "Ovambo people"), the [Ganguela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganguela_people "Ganguela people") and the [Xindonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xindonga "Xindonga")) as well as about 2% *[mulattos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulatto "Mulatto")* (mixed European and African), 1.6% Chinese and 1% European.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIA-5) The Ambundu and Ovimbundu ethnic groups combined form a majority of the population, at 62%.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-163)[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-164) However, on 23 March 2016, official data revealed by Angola's National Statistic Institute – Instituto Nacional de Estatística (INE), states that Angola has a population of 25,789,024 inhabitants.

It is estimated that Angola was host to 12,100 refugees and 2,900 asylum seekers by the end of 2007. 11,400 of those refugees were originally from the Democratic Republic of Congo, who arrived in the 1970s.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165) As of 2008[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit) there were an estimated 400,000 [Democratic Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo "Democratic Republic of the Congo") migrant workers,[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166) at least 220,000 [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Angolans "Portuguese Angolans"),[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Observat%C3%B3rio_da_Emigra%C3%A7%C3%A3o-167) and about 259,000 [Chinese living in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_people_in_Angola "Chinese people in Angola").[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-168) 1 million Angolans are [mixed race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_race "Mixed race") (black and white). Also, 40,000 [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_people "Vietnamese people") live in the country.[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-13)[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:2-16)

Since 2003, more than 400,000 [Congolese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo "Democratic Republic of the Congo") migrants have been expelled from Angola.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-169) Prior to independence in 1975, Angola had a community of approximately 350,000 Portuguese,[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-170)[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-171) but the vast majority left after independence and the ensuing civil war. However, Angola has recovered its Portuguese minority in recent years; currently, there are about 200,000 registered with the consulates, and increasing due to the debt crisis in Portugal and the relative prosperity in Angola.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-172) The Chinese population stands at 258,920, mostly composed of temporary migrants.[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-173) Also, there is a small [Brazilian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil "Brazil") community of about 5,000 people.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174) The [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people "Romani people") were deported to Angola from Portugal.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175)

As of 2007[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit), the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate "Total fertility rate") of Angola is 5.54 children born per woman (2012 estimates), the 11th highest in the world.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIA-5)

### Languages

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Languages")]

Situation of Portuguese in each province of Angola:

  Official majority language

  Official language but not majority native language

The languages in Angola are those originally spoken by the different ethnic groups and [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language "Portuguese language"), introduced during the Portuguese colonial era. The most widely spoken indigenous languages are [Umbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbundu "Umbundu"), [Kimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbundu "Kimbundu") and [Kikongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kikongo "Kikongo"), in that order. Portuguese is the official language of the country.

Although the exact numbers of those fluent in Portuguese or who speak Portuguese as a first language are unknown, a 2012 study mentions that Portuguese is the first language of 39% of the population.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177) In 2014, a census carried out by the Instituto Nacional de Estatística in Angola mentions that 71.15% of the nearly 25.8 million inhabitants of Angola (meaning around 18.3 million people) use Portuguese as a first or second language.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Angola:_portugu%C3%AAs_%C3%A9_falado_por_71,15%_de_angolanos-178)

According to the 2014 census, [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language "Portuguese language") is spoken by 71.1% of Angolans, [Umbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbundu_language "Umbundu language") by 23%, [Kikongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kikongo_language "Kikongo language") by 8.2%, [Kimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbundu "Kimbundu") by 7.8%, [Chokwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chokwe_language "Chokwe language") by 6.5%, [Nyaneka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyaneka_language "Nyaneka language") by 3.4%, [Ngangela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngangela_language "Ngangela language") by 3.1%, [Fiote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiote_language "Fiote language") by 2.4%, [Kwanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwanyama_language "Kwanyama language") by 2.3%, [Muhumbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Muhumbi_language\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Muhumbi language (page does not exist)") by 2.1%, [Luvale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luvale_language "Luvale language") by 1%, and [other languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Angola "Languages of Angola") by 4.1%.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Censo2014-179)

### Religion

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Religion")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/Igreja_Matriz_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_%282%29.jpg/220px-Igreja_Matriz_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_%282%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Igreja_Matriz_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_\(2\).jpg)

Catholic church of [Uaco Cungo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uaco_Cungo "Uaco Cungo")

There are about 1,000 religious communities, mostly [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian "Christian"), in Angola.[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180) While reliable statistics are nonexistent, estimates have it that more than half of the population are Catholics, while about a quarter adhere to the Protestant churches introduced during the colonial period: the [Congregationalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregationalist "Congregationalist") mainly among the [Ovimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovimbundu "Ovimbundu") of the Central Highlands and the coastal region to its west, the [Methodists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodists "Methodists") concentrating on the [Kimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbundu "Kimbundu") speaking strip from Luanda to Malanje, the [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists "Baptists") almost exclusively among the [Bakongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakongo "Bakongo") of the north-west (now present in Luanda as well) and dispersed [Adventists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventists "Adventists"), [Reformed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformed_tradition "Reformed tradition"), and [Lutherans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheranism "Lutheranism").[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182)

[Religion in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Angola "Religion in Angola") (2015)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183)

  [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism "Roman Catholicism") (56.4%)

  [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism "Protestantism") (23.4%)

  Other [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") (13.6%)

  [Traditional faiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_African_religions "Traditional African religions") (4.5%)

  [Irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion "Irreligion") (1%)

  Others (1.1%)

In Luanda and region there subsists a nucleus of the "[syncretic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretic "Syncretic")" [Tocoists](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tocoists\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Tocoists (page does not exist)") and in the north-west a sprinkling of [Kimbanguism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbanguism "Kimbanguism") can be found, spreading from the Congo/Zaïre. Since independence, hundreds of [Pentecostal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal "Pentecostal") and similar communities have sprung up in the cities, whereby now about 50% of the population is living; several of these communities/churches are of Brazilian origin.

As of 2008[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit) the [U.S. Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_State "U.S. Department of State") estimates the Muslim population at 80,000–90,000, less than 1% of the population,[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184) while the [Islamic Community of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Angola "Islam in Angola") puts the figure closer to 500,000.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185) Muslims consist largely of migrants from West Africa and the Middle East (especially [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon")), although some are local converts.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-customs-186) The Angolan government does not [legally recognize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Angola#Legal_status "Islam in Angola") any Muslim organizations and often shuts down mosques or prevents their construction.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-IRFR2012-187)

In a study assessing nations' levels of religious regulation and persecution with scores ranging from 0 to 10 where 0 represented low levels of regulation or persecution, Angola was scored 0.8 on Government Regulation of Religion, 4.0 on Social Regulation of Religion, 0 on Government Favoritism of Religion and 0 on Religious Persecution.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-188)

Foreign [missionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missionary "Missionary") were very active prior to independence in 1975, although since the beginning of the anti-colonial fight in 1961 the Portuguese colonial authorities expelled a series of Protestant missionaries and closed mission stations based on the belief that the missionaries were inciting pro-independence sentiments. Missionaries have been able to return to the country since the early 1990s, although security conditions due to the civil war have prevented them until 2002 from restoring many of their former inland mission stations.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-189)

The Catholic Church and some major Protestant denominations mostly keep to themselves in contrast to the "New Churches" which actively proselytize. Catholics, as well as some major Protestant denominations, provide help for the poor in the form of crop seeds, farm animals, medical care and education.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-books.google.com-190)[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191)

### Health

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Health")]

Main article: [Health in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Angola "Health in Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Maternidade_Lucr%C3%A9cia_Pa%C3%ADm_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Maternidade_Lucr%C3%A9cia_Pa%C3%ADm_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Maternidade_Lucr%C3%A9cia_Pa%C3%ADm_-_panoramio_-_Rog%C3%A9rio_Melo_\(cropped\).jpg)

Lucrécia Paím Maternity Hospital

Epidemics of [cholera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholera "Cholera"), [malaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaria "Malaria"), [rabies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabies "Rabies") and African [hemorrhagic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemorrhagic "Hemorrhagic") fevers like [Marburg hemorrhagic fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marburg_virus "Marburg virus"), are common diseases in several parts of the country. Many regions in this country have high incidence rates of [tuberculosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuberculosis "Tuberculosis") and high [HIV prevalence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Africa "HIV/AIDS in Africa") rates. [Dengue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dengue "Dengue"), [filariasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filariasis "Filariasis"), [leishmaniasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leishmaniasis "Leishmaniasis") and [onchocerciasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onchocerciasis "Onchocerciasis") (river blindness) are other diseases carried by insects that also occur in the region. Angola has one of the highest [infant mortality rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate "Infant mortality rate") in the world and one of the world's lowest [life expectancies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancies "Life expectancies"). A 2007 survey concluded that low and deficient [niacin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niacin_\(nutrient\) "Niacin (nutrient)") status was common in Angola.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192) [Demographic and Health Surveys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_and_Health_Surveys "Demographic and Health Surveys") is currently conducting several surveys in Angola on malaria, domestic violence and more.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Angola_Surveys-193)

In September 2014, the Angolan Institute for Cancer Control (IACC) was created by presidential decree, and it will integrate the National Health Service in Angola.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-194) The purpose of this new centre is to ensure health and medical care in [oncology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oncology "Oncology"), policy implementation, programmes and plans for prevention and specialised treatment.[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195) This cancer institute will be assumed as a reference institution in the central and southern regions of Africa.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196)

In 2014, Angola launched a national campaign of [vaccination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaccination "Vaccination") against [measles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Measles "Measles"), extended to every child under ten years old and aiming to go to all 18 provinces in the country.[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197) The measure is part of the Strategic Plan for the Elimination of Measles 2014–2020 created by the Angolan Ministry of Health which includes strengthening routine immunisation, a proper dealing with measles cases, national campaigns, introducing a second dose of vaccination in the national routine vaccination calendar and active epidemiological surveillance for measles. This campaign took place together with the vaccination against [polio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polio "Polio") and [vitamin A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitamin_A "Vitamin A") supplementation.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-198)

A [yellow fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Angola_and_DR_Congo_yellow_fever_outbreak "2016 Angola and DR Congo yellow fever outbreak") outbreak, the worst in the country in three decades[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-199) began in December 2015. By August 2016, when the outbreak began to subside, nearly 4,000 people were suspected of being infected. As many as 369 may have died. The outbreak began in the capital, Luanda, and spread to at least 16 of the 18 provinces. In the 2024 Global Hunger Index (GHI), Angola has a serious level of hunger and ranks 103rd out of 127 countries. Angola's GHI score is 26.6[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-200)

### Education

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Education")]

Main article: [Education in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Angola "Education in Angola")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/New_campus_%286177681401%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-New_campus_%286177681401%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:New_campus_\(6177681401\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Agostinho Neto University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agostinho_Neto_University "Agostinho Neto University")

Although by law education in Angola is compulsory and free for eight years, the government reports that a percentage of pupils are not attending due to a lack of school buildings and teachers.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Pupils are often responsible for paying additional school-related expenses, including fees for books and supplies.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201)

In 1999, the gross primary enrollment rate was 74 per cent and in 1998, the most recent year for which data are available, the net primary enrollment rate was 61 per cent.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Gross and net enrollment ratios are based on the number of pupils formally registered in primary school and therefore do not necessarily reflect actual school attendance.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) There continue to be significant disparities in enrollment between rural and urban areas. In 1995, 71.2 per cent of children ages 7 to 14 years were attending school.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) It is reported that higher percentages of boys attend school than girls.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) During the [Angolan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War") (1975–2002), nearly half of all schools were reportedly looted and destroyed, leading to current problems with overcrowding.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Waku_Kungo%2C_Angola_-_panoramio_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Waku_Kungo%2C_Angola_-_panoramio_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Waku_Kungo,_Angola_-_panoramio_\(cropped\).jpg)

A primary school in [Province of Cuanza Sul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuanza_Sul_Province "Cuanza Sul Province")

The Ministry of Education recruited 20,000 new teachers in 2005 and continued to implement teacher training.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Teachers tend to be underpaid, inadequately trained and overworked (sometimes teaching two or three shifts a day).[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Some teachers may reportedly demand payment or bribes directly from their pupils.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Other factors, such as the presence of landmines, lack of resources and identity papers, and poor health prevent children from regularly attending school.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201) Although budgetary allocations for education were increased in 2004, the education system in Angola continues to be extremely under-funded.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ilab-201)

According to estimates by the [UNESCO Institute for Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Institute_for_Statistics "UNESCO Institute for Statistics"), the adult literacy rate in 2011 was 70.4%.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-unescolit-202) By 2015, this had increased to 71.1%.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203) 82.9% of men and 54.2% of women are literate as of 2001.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204) Since independence from Portugal in 1975, a number of Angolan students continued to be admitted every year at high schools, [polytechnical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politechnic "Politechnic") institutes and universities in [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal") and [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil "Brazil") through bilateral agreements; in general, these students belong to the elites.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Lyceum_Salvador_Correia_in_Luanda%2C_Angola_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Lyceum_Salvador_Correia_in_Luanda%2C_Angola_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lyceum_Salvador_Correia_in_Luanda,_Angola_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Mutu-ya Kevela Prep. School](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magist%C3%A9rio_Mutu-ya-Kevela "pt:Magistério Mutu-ya-Kevela")

In September 2014, the Angolan Ministry of Education announced an investment of 16 million [Euros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro "Euro") in the computerisation of over 300 classrooms across the country. The project also includes training teachers at a national level, "as a way to introduce and use new information technologies in primary schools, thus reflecting an improvement in the quality of teaching".[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205)

In 2010, the Angolan government started building the Angolan Media Libraries Network, distributed throughout several provinces in the country to facilitate the people's access to information and knowledge. Each site has a bibliographic archive, multimedia resources and computers with Internet access, as well as areas for reading, researching and socialising.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) The plan envisages the establishment of one media library in each Angolan province by 2017. The project also includes the implementation of several media libraries, in order to provide the several contents available in the fixed media libraries to the most isolated populations in the country.[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207) At this time, the mobile media libraries are already operating in the provinces of Luanda, Malanje, Uíge, Cabinda and Lunda South. As for REMA, the provinces of Luanda, Benguela, Lubango and Soyo have currently working media libraries.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208)

## Culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: Culture")]

Main article: [Culture of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Angola "Culture of Angola")

See also: [Music of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Angola "Music of Angola") and [Angolan cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_cuisine "Angolan cuisine")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Memorial_Antonio_Agostinho_Neto_%2819882325368%29.jpg/170px-Memorial_Antonio_Agostinho_Neto_%2819882325368%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Memorial_Antonio_Agostinho_Neto_\(19882325368\).jpg)

[Agostinho Neto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agostinho_Neto "Agostinho Neto") National Memorial in Luanda

Angolan culture has been heavily influenced by [Portuguese culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_culture "Portuguese culture"), especially in language and religion, and the culture of the indigenous ethnic groups of Angola, predominantly [Bantu culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_people "Bantu people").

The diverse ethnic communities—the [Ovimbundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovimbundu "Ovimbundu"), [Ambundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Mbundu_people "Northern Mbundu people"), [Bakongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakongo "Bakongo"), [Chokwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chokwe_people "Chokwe people"), [Mbunda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mbunda_people "Mbunda people") and other peoples—to varying degrees maintain their own cultural traits, traditions and languages, but in the cities, where slightly more than half of the population now lives, a mixed culture has been emerging since colonial times; in [Luanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luanda "Luanda"), since its foundation in the 16th century.

In this urban culture, Portuguese heritage has become more and more dominant. African roots are evident in music and dance and is moulding the way in which Portuguese is spoken. This process is well reflected in contemporary Angolan literature, especially in the works of [Angolan authors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_literature "Angolan literature").

In 2014, Angola resumed the National Festival of Angolan Culture after a 25-year break. The festival took place in all the provincial capitals and lasted for 20 days, with the theme "Culture as a Factor of Peace and Development.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-209)

### Media

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Media")]

Main articles: [Mass media in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Angola "Mass media in Angola"), [List of newspapers in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Angola "List of newspapers in Angola"), and [Television in Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Angola "Television in Angola")

### Cinema

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Cinema")]

Main article: [Cinema of Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Angola "Cinema of Angola")

In 1972, one of Angola's first feature films, [Sarah Maldoror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Maldoror "Sarah Maldoror")'s [internationally co-produced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-production_\(media\) "Co-production (media)") *[Sambizanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambizanga_\(film\) "Sambizanga (film)")*, was released at the [Carthage Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carthage_Film_Festival "Carthage Film Festival") to critical acclaim, winning the *Tanit d'Or*, the festival's highest prize.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-210)

### Sports

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Angola\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Sports")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Est%C3%A1dio_Nacional_de_Ombaka_%2819151153514%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Est%C3%A1dio_Nacional_de_Ombaka_%2819151153514%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Est%C3%A1dio_Nacional_de_Ombaka_\(19151153514\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

The National Stadium in [Benguela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benguela "Benguela")

[Basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball "Basketball") is the second most popular sport in Angola. Its [national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola_national_basketball_team "Angola national basketball team") has won the [AfroBasket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfroBasket "AfroBasket") 11 times and holds the record of most titles. As a top team in Africa, it is a regular competitor at the [Summer Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_Summer_Olympics "Basketball at the Summer Olympics") and the [FIBA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_World_Cup "FIBA World Cup"). Angola is home to one of Africa's first competitive leagues.[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-211) [Bruno Fernando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Fernando "Bruno Fernando"), a player for the [Atlanta Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Hawks "Atlanta Hawks"), is the only current [NBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA "NBA") player from Angola.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In football, Angola hosted the [2010 Africa Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Africa_Cup_of_Nations "2010 Africa Cup of Nations"). The [Angola national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola_national_football_team "Angola national football team") qualified for the [2006 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup "2006 FIFA World Cup"), their first appearance in the World Cup finals. They were eliminated after one defeat and two draws in the group stage. They won three [COSAFA Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COSAFA_Cup "COSAFA Cup") and finished runner-up in the [2011 African Nations Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_African_Nations_Championship "2011 African Nations Championship").

Angola has participated in the [World Women's Handball Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Women%27s_Handball_Championship "World Women's Handball Championship") for several years. The country has also appeared in the [Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola_at_the_Olympics "Angola at the Olympics") for seven years and both regularly competes in and once has hosted the [FIRS Roller Hockey World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIRS_Roller_Hockey_World_Cup "FIRS Roller Hockey World Cup"), where the best finish is sixth. Angola is also often believed to have historic roots in the [martial art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_art "Martial art") "[Capoeira Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capoeira_Angola "Capoeira Angola")" and "[Batuque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batuque_\(game\) "Batuque (game)")" which were practised by [enslaved African Angolans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_Angola "Slavery in Angola") transported as part of the [Atlantic slave trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_slave_trade "Atlantic slave trade").[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-212)
